=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n=len(edges)
        bl=[0]*n
        mp=defaultdict(int)
        mx=-1
        for i in range(n:
            if(bl[i]==0):
                x=i
                l=0
                st=set()
                while x>-1 and bl[x]==0:
                    bl[x]=0
                    mp[x]=l
                    l+=1
                    st.add(x)
                    x=edges[x]
                if(x!=-1 and x in st): mx=max(mx,l-mp[x])
        return mx

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(edges)
        visited = [0] * n
        ans = -1

        for i in range(n):
            if visited[i]:
                continue

            pos = {}
            cur = i
            dist = 0

            while cur != -1 and not visited[cur]:
                visited[cur] = 1
                pos[cur] = dist
                dist += 1
                cur = edges[cur]

            if cur != -1 and cur in pos:
                ans = max(ans, dist - pos[cur])

        return ans
```

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(edges)
        visited = [0] * n
        ans = -1

        for i in range(n):
            if visited[i]:
                continue

            pos = {}
            cur = i
            dist = 0

            while cur != -1 and not visited[cur]:
                visited[cur] = 1
                pos[cur] = dist
                dist += 1
                cur = edges[cur]

            if cur != -1 and cur in pos:
                ans = max(ans, dist - pos[cur])

        return ans
```

Need maybe if there is a path that starts at a node, reaches a previously visited node that is in a cycle, and current path's nodes could form a different cycle by connecting to that node? As argued, no because if current path nodes were reachable from the cycle node, they'd have been visited. Let's formalize: Suppose current path a0 -> a1 -> ... -> ak = b, where b is in previous path. If there were a cycle involving some current path nodes and b, then from b following edges must eventually reach one of current path nodes, say aj. But previous traversal from b followed the same deterministic edges and would have visited aj. So aj would be visited before current traversal, contradiction to aj unvisited at start. Thus no new cycle. Good.

Let's ensure indentation correct.

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(edges)
        visited = [0] * n
        ans = -1

        for i in range(n):
            if visited[i]:
                continue

            pos = {}
            cur = i
            dist = 0

            while cur != -1 and not visited[cur]:
                visited[cur] = 1
                pos[cur] = dist
                dist += 1
                cur = edges[cur]

            if cur != -1 and cur in pos:
                ans = max(ans, dist - pos[cur])

        return ans
```

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(edges)
        visited = [0] * n
        ans = -1

        for i in range(n):
            if visited[i]:
                continue

            pos = {}
            cur = i
            dist = 0

            while cur != -1 and not visited[cur]:
                visited[cur] = 1
                pos[cur] = dist
                dist += 1
                cur = edges[cur]

            if cur != -1 and cur in pos:
                ans = max(ans, dist - pos[cur])

        return ans
